## common/cognitive-complexity

**Cognitive complexity of functions should not be too high.** Deeply nested and heavily branched functions are hard to understand and change. Cognitive complexity adds 1 per branch, loop, catch or boolean-operator sequence, plus the current nesting depth for nested structures. Threshold: `thresholds.cognitive_complexity`.

SonarQube: S3776

## common/function-length

**Functions should not be too long.** Long functions usually do too many things. Threshold: `thresholds.function_lines`.

SonarQube: S138

## common/too-many-parameters

**Functions should not have too many parameters.** Many parameters make calls hard to read; group them into an object. Threshold: `thresholds.parameters`.

SonarQube: S107

## common/todo-comment

**Track TODO and FIXME comments.** TODO, FIXME and XXX comments mark unfinished work that should be tracked.

SonarQube: S1134, S1135, S1707 (related)

## common/trailing-whitespace

**Lines should not end with trailing whitespaces.** Trailing spaces and tabs are invisible noise in diffs.

Off by default: add `"common/trailing-whitespace"` to `rules.enabled` to run it.

SonarQube: S1131

## common/line-length

**Lines should not be too long.** Lines past 120 characters are hard to read side by side and in reviews.

Off by default: add `"common/line-length"` to `rules.enabled` to run it.

SonarQube: S103, LineLength

## common/file-length

**Files should not have too many lines of code.** A file of more than 1000 lines of code usually holds several responsibilities.

Off by default: add `"common/file-length"` to `rules.enabled` to run it.

SonarQube: S104

## common/final-newline

**Files should end with a newline.** POSIX tools treat a file without a final newline as having an incomplete last line.

Off by default: add `"common/final-newline"` to `rules.enabled` to run it.

SonarQube: S113

## common/commented-out-code

**Sections of code should not be commented out.** Commented-out code goes stale and confuses readers; version control keeps the history.

SonarQube: S125

## common/tab-character

**Tabulation characters should not be used.** Tabs show differently in every editor, so the same file reads differently for each reader.

Off by default: add `"common/tab-character"` to `rules.enabled` to run it.

SonarQube: S105

## common/trailing-comment

**Comments should not be located at the end of lines of code.** A comment after code pushes the line out and is easy to miss.

Off by default: add `"common/trailing-comment"` to `rules.enabled` to run it.

SonarQube: S139

## common/nosonar-comment

**Track uses of NOSONAR comments.** A NOSONAR comment silences every issue on its line, so each one is worth a review.

Off by default: add `"common/nosonar-comment"` to `rules.enabled` to run it.

SonarQube: NoSonar, S1291

## java/package-directory

**Package declaration should match source file directory.** A package that does not match the directory breaks tools and surprises readers. Java files only.

Off by default: add `"java/package-directory"` to `rules.enabled` to run it.

SonarQube: S1598

## java/test-in-production-source

**Tests should be kept in a dedicated source directory.** Test classes under src/main ship with the application. Java files only.

Off by default: add `"java/test-in-production-source"` to `rules.enabled` to run it.

SonarQube: S3414

## java/assertion-in-production

**Assertions should not be used in production code.** Test assertion libraries imported under src/main end up in production. Java files only.

SonarQube: S5960

## js/module-imports-itself

**Module should not import itself.** A module that imports its own file gets a half-initialized copy of itself. JavaScript and TypeScript files only.

SonarQube: S7060

## js/default-export-name

**Default export names and file names should match.** A default export named differently from its file is hard to find. JavaScript and TypeScript files only.

Off by default: add `"js/default-export-name"` to `rules.enabled` to run it.

SonarQube: S3317

## js/test-file-without-tests

**Test files should contain at least one test case.** A file named like a test that defines no test never runs anything. JavaScript and TypeScript files only.

SonarQube: S2187

## js/dependency-not-declared

**Dependencies should be explicit.** A package imported but missing from the nearest package.json works only because something else installed it. JavaScript and TypeScript files only.

Off by default: add `"js/dependency-not-declared"` to `rules.enabled` to run it.

SonarQube: S4328

## js/missing-license-header

**Track lack of copyright and license headers.** Files should start with a comment naming the copyright holder or license. JavaScript and TypeScript files only.

Off by default: add `"js/missing-license-header"` to `rules.enabled` to run it.

SonarQube: S1451

## java/parse-failure

**Java parser failure.** The file could not be parsed completely, so rules on it may miss issues. Java files only.

Off by default: add `"java/parse-failure"` to `rules.enabled` to run it.

SonarQube: S2260

## java/package-info-missing

**Packages should have a javadoc file package-info.java.** package-info.java is where a package is documented. Java projects only.

Off by default: add `"java/package-info-missing"` to `rules.enabled` to run it.

SonarQube: S1228

## java/package-info-only

**Packages containing only package-info.java should be removed.** A package with nothing but its documentation file has no code. Java projects only.

SonarQube: S4032

## java/component-not-scanned

**Spring beans should be considered by @ComponentScan.** A component outside every scanned base package is never registered. Java projects only.

Off by default: add `"java/component-not-scanned"` to `rules.enabled` to run it.

SonarQube: S4605

## cs/partial-method-not-implemented

**Partial methods should be implemented.** The compiler silently removes calls to a partial method that no file of the project implements, so the intended behavior never runs. C# projects only.

SonarQube: S3251

## cs/neutral-resources-language

**Assemblies with resources should declare their neutral language.** NeutralResourcesLanguage tells the ResourceManager which culture the default resources are in, which saves a lookup. Checked in classic AssemblyInfo files of projects that use a ResourceManager. C# projects only.

Off by default: add `"cs/neutral-resources-language"` to `rules.enabled` to run it.

SonarQube: S4026 (related)

## js/no-eval

**eval() should not be used.** eval() executes arbitrary strings as code and is a common injection vector.

SonarQube: S1523 (related)

Standards: [CWE-95](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/95.html), OWASP A05:2025 Injection, OWASP A03:2021 Injection

Noncompliant:

```javascript
eval(userInput);
```

Compliant:

```javascript
JSON.parse(userInput);
```

## js/no-new-function

**The Function constructor should not be used.** new Function(...) compiles strings into code, just like eval().

SonarQube: S1523 (related)

Standards: [CWE-95](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/95.html), OWASP A05:2025 Injection, OWASP A03:2021 Injection

Noncompliant:

```javascript
const f = new Function('a', 'return a');
```

Compliant:

```javascript
const f = (a) => a;
```

## js/inner-html

**HTML should not be assigned through innerHTML/outerHTML.** Assigning strings to innerHTML or outerHTML can lead to cross-site scripting.

Standards: [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html), OWASP A05:2025 Injection, OWASP A03:2021 Injection

Noncompliant:

```javascript
el.innerHTML = html;
```

Compliant:

```javascript
el.textContent = text;
```

## js/eqeqeq

**Strict equality operators should be used.** `==` and `!=` perform type coercion with surprising results. Comparisons against `null`/`undefined` are allowed.

SonarQube: S1440

Noncompliant:

```javascript
if (a == b) { f(); }
```

Compliant:

```javascript
if (a === b) { f(); }
if (x == null) { g(); }
```

## js/assignment-in-condition

**Assignments should not be used as conditions.** `if (x = y)` is almost always a typo for `===`. Wrap in extra parentheses if intended.

SonarQube: S1121 (related)

Noncompliant:

```javascript
if (x = 5) { f(); }
```

Compliant:

```javascript
if (x === 5) { f(); }
if ((m = re.exec(s))) { g(); }
```

## js/no-debugger

**debugger statements should not be committed.** debugger statements pause execution in any open devtools.

SonarQube: S1525

Noncompliant:

```javascript
function f() { debugger; }
```

Compliant:

```javascript
function f() { return 1; }
```

## js/no-var

**let or const should be used instead of var.** var is function-scoped and hoisted, which causes subtle bugs.

SonarQube: S3504

Noncompliant:

```javascript
var x = 1;
```

Compliant:

```javascript
let x = 1;
const y = 2;
```

## js/empty-catch

**Exceptions should not be silently swallowed.** An empty catch block hides failures. Handle the error or explain with a comment.

SonarQube: S2486 (related)

Noncompliant:

```javascript
try { f(); } catch (e) {}
```

Compliant:

```javascript
try { f(); } catch (e) { /* best effort */ }
```

## js/no-console

**console calls should not be left in production code.** Use a proper logger instead of console.* calls.

Noncompliant:

```javascript
console.log('hi');
```

Compliant:

```javascript
logger.info('hi');
```

## js/jump-in-finally

**finally blocks should not return, throw, break or continue.** A jump out of a finally block discards whatever the try or catch block was doing: its return value or its exception is silently lost.

SonarQube: S1143

Noncompliant:

```javascript
function f() { try { return g(); } finally { return 0; } }
```

Compliant:

```javascript
function f() { try { return g(); } finally { for (;;) { break; } cleanup(); } }
```

## js/no-with

**with statements should not be used.** Inside `with`, a bare name may refer to a property of the object or to a variable, depending on the object at run time. Strict mode forbids it.

SonarQube: S1321

Noncompliant:

```javascript
with (config) { f(port); }
```

Compliant:

```javascript
const c = config; f(c.port);
```

## js/duplicate-parameter

**Parameter names should be unique.** When two parameters share a name, the later one hides the earlier, whose argument becomes unreachable.

SonarQube: S1536

Noncompliant:

```javascript
function move(x, y, x) { return x + y; }
```

Compliant:

```javascript
function move(x, y, z) { return x + y + z; }
```

## js/self-assignment

**Variables should not be assigned to themselves.** `x = x` has no effect. It usually means another variable or property was meant.

SonarQube: S1656

Noncompliant:

```javascript
this.name = this.name;
```

Compliant:

```javascript
this.name = name;
items[i++] = items[i++];
```

## js/identical-operands

**Both sides of an operator should not be identical.** `a === a`, `a - a` or `a && a` always give the same result, so one side is almost always a copy-paste mistake. `x !== x` (a NaN test) and `h & h` (a 32-bit integer conversion) are idioms and allowed.

SonarQube: S1764

Noncompliant:

```javascript
if (a.length === a.length) { f(); }
```

Compliant:

```javascript
if (a.length === b.length) { f(); }
const r = next() - next();
const m = 1 << 1;
```

## js/duplicate-condition

**Conditions in an if/else-if chain or switch should be unique.** A condition repeated further down an if/else-if chain, or a repeated switch case, can never be reached: the first one always wins.

SonarQube: S1862

Noncompliant:

```javascript
if (x === 1) { a(); } else if (x === 2) { b(); } else if (x === 1) { c(); }
```

Compliant:

```javascript
if (x === 1) { a(); } else if (x === 2) { b(); }
switch (y) { case 1: a(); break; case 2: b(); }
```

## js/nan-comparison

**NaN should not be compared with operators.** NaN is not equal to anything, itself included, so `x === NaN` is always false.

SonarQube: S2688

Noncompliant:

```javascript
if (value === NaN) { reset(); }
```

Compliant:

```javascript
if (Number.isNaN(value)) { reset(); }
```

## js/reversed-compound-assignment

**'=+', '=-' and '=!' should not be used.** `x =+ 1` assigns `+1` to `x`; it was almost certainly meant as `x += 1`.

SonarQube: S2757

Noncompliant:

```javascript
total =+ price;
```

Compliant:

```javascript
total += price;
sign = -1;
let ready = !busy;
```

## js/delete-variable

**delete should only be used on object properties.** `delete` removes properties. On a variable it does nothing (and is a syntax error in strict mode).

SonarQube: S3001

Noncompliant:

```javascript
delete cache;
```

Compliant:

```javascript
delete cache[key];
cache = undefined;
```

## js/case-with-comma-or-or

**switch cases should not combine values with ',' or '||'.** `case 1, 2:` matches only 2, and `case a || b:` matches only the value of `a || b`. Neither matches any of the listed values.

SonarQube: S3616

Noncompliant:

```javascript
switch (day) { case 'sat', 'sun': rest(); }
```

Compliant:

```javascript
switch (day) { case 'sat':
case 'sun': rest(); }
```

## js/empty-destructuring

**Destructuring patterns should not be empty.** `const {} = obj` binds nothing. A default value was usually meant: `const { a = {} } = obj`.

SonarQube: S3799

Noncompliant:

```javascript
const { options: {} } = settings;
```

Compliant:

```javascript
const { options = {} } = settings;
const [, second] = pair;
```

## js/negated-in-instanceof

**Negated 'in' and 'instanceof' operands should be parenthesized.** `!key in obj` negates `key` first and then tests `false in obj`; `!a instanceof B` is always false.

SonarQube: S3812

Noncompliant:

```javascript
if (!err instanceof TypeError) { retry(); }
```

Compliant:

```javascript
if (!(err instanceof TypeError)) { retry(); }
```

## js/negated-equality-operand

**A negated operand should not be compared for equality.** `!a === b` compares the boolean `!a` with `b`, not `a` with `b`.

SonarQube: S7736

Noncompliant:

```javascript
if (!status === 'ok') { warn(); }
```

Compliant:

```javascript
if (status !== 'ok') { warn(); }
const same = !a === !b;
if (!0 === x || !!y === z) {}
```

## js/new-symbol-bigint

**Symbol and BigInt should not be called with new.** Symbol() and BigInt() are functions, not constructors: `new` throws a TypeError.

SonarQube: S3834

Noncompliant:

```javascript
const id = new Symbol('id');
```

Compliant:

```javascript
const id = Symbol('id');
```

## js/invalid-typeof

**typeof should be compared with a type it can return.** typeof only returns "undefined", "object", "boolean", "number", "bigint", "string", "symbol" or "function"; a comparison with anything else is always false.

SonarQube: S4125

Noncompliant:

```javascript
if (typeof x === 'array') { f(); }
```

Compliant:

```javascript
if (typeof x === 'object') { f(); }
if ('string' === typeof y) { g(); }
```

## js/setter-return

**Setters should not return a value.** The value returned by a setter is discarded: `o.x = v` always evaluates to `v`.

SonarQube: S2432

Noncompliant:

```javascript
class A { set x(v) { this._x = v; return this; } }
```

Compliant:

```javascript
class A { set x(v) { if (v == null) { return; } this._x = v; this.on(() => { return 1; }); } }
```

## js/generator-without-yield

**Generators should yield.** A generator that never yields produces an empty sequence; it was probably meant to be a plain function, or a yield is missing.

SonarQube: S3531

Noncompliant:

```javascript
function* ids() { return [1, 2, 3]; }
```

Compliant:

```javascript
function* ids() { yield* [1, 2, 3]; }
function* none() {}
```

## js/duplicate-member

**Object literals and classes should not define a member twice.** When a key or member name repeats, the last definition silently replaces the earlier ones.

SonarQube: S1534

Noncompliant:

```javascript
const o = { port: 80, host: 'a', 'port': 8080 };
```

Compliant:

```javascript
const o = { port: 80, host: 'a', get v() { return 1; }, set v(x) {} };
class A { static of() {} of() {} }
```

## js/useless-increment

**A variable should not be assigned its own post-increment.** `i = i++` stores the old value back into `i`, so the increment is lost.

SonarQube: S2123

Noncompliant:

```javascript
count = count++;
```

Compliant:

```javascript
count++;
prev = count++;
```

## js/for-wrong-direction

**A for loop should move its counter toward the end condition.** `for (i = 0; i < n; i--)` moves away from its bound, so the loop never ends or ends only on overflow.

SonarQube: S2251

Noncompliant:

```javascript
for (let i = 0; i < n; i--) { f(i); }
```

Compliant:

```javascript
for (let i = 0; i < n; i++) { f(i); }
for (let i = n; i > 0; i -= 2) { f(i); }
```

## js/identical-branches

**Every branch of a conditional should not do the same thing.** When all branches of an if/else chain or a ternary are identical, the condition is pointless, or one branch was meant to differ.

SonarQube: S3923

Noncompliant:

```javascript
if (fast) { run(); } else { run(); }
```

Compliant:

```javascript
if (fast) { run(); } else { walk(); }
if (a) { run(); } else if (b) { run(); }
```

## js/error-not-thrown

**Errors should be thrown, not just created.** `new Error(...)` on its own line creates an error and drops it. `throw` was almost certainly forgotten.

SonarQube: S3984

Noncompliant:

```javascript
if (!ok) { new TypeError('bad input'); }
```

Compliant:

```javascript
if (!ok) { throw new TypeError('bad input'); }
```

## js/literal-called

**Literals should not be called as functions.** Calling a string, number or other literal always throws a TypeError. It usually comes from a missing semicolon before a parenthesized line.

SonarQube: S6958

Noncompliant:

```javascript
const s = 'a'
(items || []).forEach(f);
```

Compliant:

```javascript
const s = 'a';
(items || []).forEach(f);
const q = sql`select 1`;
```

## js/exclusive-test

**Exclusive tests should not be committed.** `it.only` and `describe.only` silently skip every other test in the run, so CI stays green while tests are ignored.

SonarQube: S6426

Noncompliant:

```javascript
it.only('adds', () => { add(1, 2); });
```

Compliant:

```javascript
it('adds', () => { add(1, 2); });
test.skip('later', () => {});
```

## js/shadowed-builtin-name

**undefined, NaN, Infinity, eval and arguments should not be redefined.** Binding or assigning these names makes code that relies on their standard meaning misbehave. Strict mode rejects most such uses.

SonarQube: S2137

Noncompliant:

```javascript
function f(arguments) { let undefined = 1; return arguments; }
```

Compliant:

```javascript
function f(...args) { const n = arguments.length; return undefined === n || isNaN(n); }
```

## js/tls-unverified

**TLS certificate checks should not be turned off.** `rejectUnauthorized: false` accepts any certificate, so anyone on the network path can impersonate the server.

SonarQube: S4830

Standards: [CWE-295](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/295.html), OWASP A07:2025 Authentication Failures, OWASP A02:2021 Cryptographic Failures, OWASP A05:2021 Security Misconfiguration, OWASP A07:2021 Identification and Authentication Failures

Noncompliant:

```javascript
https.request({ host, rejectUnauthorized: false });
```

Compliant:

```javascript
https.request({ host, ca: fs.readFileSync('ca.pem') });
```

## js/weak-hash

**Weak hashing algorithms should not be used.** MD2, MD4, MD5, SHA-1 and RIPEMD-160 are broken for security purposes.

SonarQube: S4790

Standards: [CWE-1240](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1240.html), OWASP A04:2025 Cryptographic Failures, OWASP A02:2021 Cryptographic Failures

Noncompliant:

```javascript
crypto.createHash('md5').update(password);
```

Compliant:

```javascript
crypto.createHash('sha256').update(data);
```

## js/number-precision-loss

**Number literals should not lose precision.** Numbers are 64-bit floats: a literal with more precision than that, like 9007199254740993, silently becomes a different number.

SonarQube: S6534

Noncompliant:

```javascript
const id = 9007199254740993;
```

Compliant:

```javascript
const id = 9007199254740993n;
const max = 9007199254740991;
const x = 0.1 + 1e21 + 0xFF;
```

## js/empty-block

**Blocks should not be left empty.** An empty if, else, loop, try or finally block is usually unfinished code. If it's intended, a comment saying why makes that clear.

SonarQube: S108

Noncompliant:

```javascript
if (ready) {} else { start(); }
```

Compliant:

```javascript
if (ready) { /* already started */ } else { start(); }
function noop() {}
```

## js/switch-fallthrough

**switch cases should not fall through.** A case without a break, return or throw at its end also runs the next case's code, which is rarely intended. Empty cases that share a body are fine, and so is a case followed by a comment that mentions falling through.

SonarQube: S128

Noncompliant:

```javascript
switch (x) { case 1: a(); case 2: b(); break; }
```

Compliant:

```javascript
switch (x) {
case 1:
case 2: b(); break;
case 3: c();
// falls through
case 4: if (y) { return; } else { throw e; }
default: d();
}
```

## js/equality-in-for-condition

**A for loop should not end on an equality test of its counter.** `for (i = 0; i !== 10; i += 3)` steps over 10 and never stops. A range test (`<`, `>`) ends the loop even when the counter skips the exact value.

SonarQube: S888

Noncompliant:

```javascript
for (let i = 0; i !== 10; i += 3) { f(i); }
```

Compliant:

```javascript
for (let i = 0; i < 10; i += 3) { f(i); }
for (let n = head; n !== null; n = n.next) { f(n); }
```

## js/no-label

**Labels should not be used.** Labeled breaks and continues jump across nested code and make control flow hard to follow. Extracting a function with an early return is usually clearer.

SonarQube: S1119

Noncompliant:

```javascript
outer: for (const a of rows) { for (const b of a) { if (b) { break outer; } } }
```

Compliant:

```javascript
for (const a of rows) { if (a.some(Boolean)) { break; } }
```

## js/boolean-literal-comparison

**Booleans should not be compared with true or false.** `x == true` is a roundabout `x`, and with loose equality it can also match values that aren't booleans.

SonarQube: S1125 (related)

Noncompliant:

```javascript
if (done == true) { stop(); }
```

Compliant:

```javascript
if (done) { stop(); }
if (flag === false) { start(); }
```

## js/return-boolean-if

**A condition should be returned directly, not through if/else.** `if (c) { return true; } else { return false; }` is a long way to write `return c;`.

SonarQube: S1126 (related)

Noncompliant:

```javascript
function big(a) { if (a > 10) { return true; } else { return false; } }
```

Compliant:

```javascript
function big(a) { return a > 10; }
function g(a) { if (a) { return true; } else { return a === 0; } }
```

## js/empty-function

**Functions should not be empty.** An empty function may be unfinished. If it's an intentional no-op, a comment inside says so. Arrow functions (common as callbacks) and constructors are not checked.

SonarQube: S1186

Noncompliant:

```javascript
class Cache { clear() {} }
```

Compliant:

```javascript
class Cache { clear() { /* nothing cached yet */ } constructor() {} }
const noop = () => {};
```

## js/nested-block

**Code blocks should not be nested for no reason.** A bare `{ ... }` inside other code adds indentation and suggests a missing if or loop. Blocks inside switch cases, which scope `let` and `const`, are fine.

SonarQube: S1199

Noncompliant:

```javascript
function f() { a(); { b(); } }
```

Compliant:

```javascript
function f() { a(); b(); }
switch (x) { case 1: { const y = 1; f(y); } }
```

## js/label-in-switch

**switch statements should not contain labels.** A label inside a switch is usually a misspelled `default:` or a missing `case`, and it silently matches nothing. Labeled blocks and loops, which a `break label` can target, are fine.

SonarQube: S1219

Noncompliant:

```javascript
switch (x) { case 1: f(); break; defualt: g(); }
```

Compliant:

```javascript
switch (x) { case 1: f(); break; default: g(); }
switch (y) { case 2: done: if (z) { break done; } }
```

## js/for-as-while

**A for loop with only a condition should be a while loop.** `for (; cond;)` has no initializer or update, which is exactly what `while (cond)` expresses more plainly.

SonarQube: S1264

Noncompliant:

```javascript
for (; i < n;) { i = next(i); }
```

Compliant:

```javascript
while (i < n) { i = next(i); }
for (;;) { if (done()) { break; } }
```

## js/octal-literal

**Legacy octal literals should not be used.** `0755` is octal, not 755, which surprises readers. Strict mode forbids it; `0o755` says it explicitly.

SonarQube: S1314

Noncompliant:

```javascript
const mode = 0755;
```

Compliant:

```javascript
const mode = 0o755;
const zero = 0;
const half = 0.5;
```

## js/octal-escape

**Octal escape sequences should not be used.** `"\251"` is an octal escape, deprecated and forbidden in strict mode and template literals.

SonarQube: S6657

Noncompliant:

```javascript
const c = '\251 2026';
```

Compliant:

```javascript
const c = '\u00a9 2026';
const nul = '\0';
```

## js/call-arguments-new-line

**Call arguments should start on the same line as the function.** `a\n(b)` is parsed as the call `a(b)`, which is rarely what was meant when a line starts with a parenthesis.

SonarQube: S1472

Noncompliant:

```javascript
const x = load
(config);
```

Compliant:

```javascript
const x = load(
  config,
);
const y = tag
`text`;
```

## js/too-many-cases

**switch statements should not have too many cases.** A switch with more than 30 cases is hard to read and maintain; a lookup table (an object or a Map) is usually clearer.

SonarQube: S1479

Noncompliant:

```javascript
switch (x) { case 0: case 1: case 2: case 3: case 4: case 5: case 6: case 7: case 8: case 9: case 10: case 11: case 12: case 13: case 14: case 15: case 16: case 17: case 18: case 19: case 20: case 21: case 22: case 23: case 24: case 25: case 26: case 27: case 28: case 29: case 30: f(); }
```

Compliant:

```javascript
switch (x) { case 0: case 1: f(); break; default: g(); }
```

## js/multiline-string

**Strings should not continue on the next line with a backslash.** A backslash at the end of a line continues the string, but any space after it breaks the code, and the indentation of the next line ends up in the string.

SonarQube: S1516

Noncompliant:

```javascript
const s = 'one \
two';
```

Compliant:

```javascript
const s = 'one ' +
  'two';
const t = `one
two`;
```

## js/primitive-wrapper

**Primitive wrapper objects should not be created.** `new String('a')` is an object, not a string: `typeof` says "object", it's always truthy (even `new Boolean(false)`), and `===` compares identity.

SonarQube: S1533

Noncompliant:

```javascript
const ok = new Boolean(false);
```

Compliant:

```javascript
const ok = Boolean(value);
const s = String(n);
```

## js/default-param-not-last

**Parameters with defaults should come last.** A default followed by a required parameter can only be used by passing `undefined` explicitly.

SonarQube: S1788

Noncompliant:

```javascript
function connect(port = 80, host) {}
```

Compliant:

```javascript
function connect(host, port = 80) {}
function g(a = 1, ...rest) {}
```

## js/inverted-equality

**Equality checks should not be inverted.** `!(a === b)` is `a !== b` with an extra step.

SonarQube: S1940 (related)

Noncompliant:

```javascript
if (!(a === b)) { f(); }
```

Compliant:

```javascript
if (a !== b) { f(); }
if (!(a < b)) { f(); }
if (!(e instanceof E)) { f(); }
```

## js/empty-class

**Classes should not be empty.** A class with no members does nothing a plain object couldn't. Classes that extend or implement something, or carry decorators, are fine.

SonarQube: S2094

Noncompliant:

```javascript
class Marker {}
```

Compliant:

```javascript
class Marker { id = 1; }
class NotFound extends Error {}
```

## js/arguments-callee

**arguments.callee and arguments.caller should not be used.** Both are forbidden in strict mode and prevent engine optimizations.

SonarQube: S2685

Noncompliant:

```javascript
function fact(n) { return n ? n * arguments.callee(n - 1) : 1; }
```

Compliant:

```javascript
function fact(n) { return n ? n * fact(n - 1) : 1; }
const c = opts.callee;
```

## js/useless-catch

**catch clauses should do more than rethrow.** `catch (e) { throw e; }` changes nothing: the error would propagate the same way without it.

SonarQube: S2737

Noncompliant:

```javascript
try { f(); } catch (e) { throw e; }
```

Compliant:

```javascript
try { f(); } catch (e) { log(e); throw e; }
try { f(); } catch (e) { throw new Wrapped(e); }
```

## js/nested-ternary

**Ternary operators should not be nested.** `a ? x : b ? y : z` packs several decisions into one expression; an if/else chain or a lookup is easier to read.

SonarQube: S3358

Noncompliant:

```javascript
const size = n > 100 ? 'big' : n > 10 ? 'medium' : 'small';
```

Compliant:

```javascript
const size = n > 100 ? 'big' : 'small';
const f = a ? g(() => (b ? 1 : 2)) : 3;
```

## js/redundant-jump

**Jumps should not be redundant.** A bare `return;` at the end of a function, or `continue;` at the end of a loop body, does nothing.

SonarQube: S3626

Noncompliant:

```javascript
function f() { g(); return; }
```

Compliant:

```javascript
function f() { if (a) { return; } g(); }
for (const x of xs) { if (x) { continue; } g(x); }
```

## js/throw-literal

**Literals should not be thrown.** A thrown string or number has no stack trace, and code that catches it can't rely on `error.message` or `instanceof Error`.

SonarQube: S3696

Noncompliant:

```javascript
throw 'not found';
```

Compliant:

```javascript
throw new Error('not found');
throw err;
```

## js/sparse-array

**Array literals should not have holes.** `[1, , 3]` creates an empty slot that iteration methods skip, and the extra comma is easy to miss or mistake for a typo.

SonarQube: S4140

Noncompliant:

```javascript
const a = [1, , 3];
```

Compliant:

```javascript
const a = [1, undefined, 3];
const b = [1, 2,];
```

## js/default-not-last

**The default clause should be last.** A `default` in the middle of a switch reads like the end of it; readers expect it last.

SonarQube: S4524

Noncompliant:

```javascript
switch (x) { default: f(); break; case 1: g(); }
```

Compliant:

```javascript
switch (x) { case 1: g(); break; default: f(); }
```

## js/lonely-if

**An if should not be the only statement in an else block.** `else { if (b) { ... } }` is `else if (b) { ... }` with an extra level of nesting.

SonarQube: S6660

Noncompliant:

```javascript
if (a) { f(); } else { if (b) { g(); } }
```

Compliant:

```javascript
if (a) { f(); } else if (b) { g(); }
if (a) { f(); } else { if (b) { g(); } h(); }
```

## js/declaration-in-case

**case clauses should not declare let, const, class or function directly.** Declarations in one case are visible in the whole switch, but only initialized when that case runs, so using them from another case throws.

SonarQube: S6836

Noncompliant:

```javascript
switch (x) { case 1: const y = 2; f(y); }
```

Compliant:

```javascript
switch (x) { case 1: { const y = 2; f(y); } }
```

## js/rename-to-same-name

**Imports, exports and destructuring should not rename to the same name.** `import { a as a }` or `const { k: k } = o` renames nothing.

SonarQube: S6650

Noncompliant:

```javascript
import { load as load } from './io';
```

Compliant:

```javascript
import { load as read } from './io';
const { k } = o;
const { k: key } = o;
```

## js/proto-property

**__proto__ should not be used.** `__proto__` is a legacy accessor. Object.getPrototypeOf, Object.setPrototypeOf and Object.create say the same thing in standard terms.

SonarQube: S6654

Noncompliant:

```javascript
const p = obj.__proto__;
```

Compliant:

```javascript
const p = Object.getPrototypeOf(obj);
```

## js/global-isnan

**Number.isNaN() should be used instead of isNaN().** The global isNaN() converts its argument first, so `isNaN('abc')` is true. Number.isNaN() only says whether the value is NaN.

SonarQube: S6679

Noncompliant:

```javascript
if (isNaN(value)) { reset(); }
```

Compliant:

```javascript
if (Number.isNaN(value)) { reset(); }
```

## js/eslint-disable-all

**eslint-disable comments should name the rules they disable.** A bare `eslint-disable` turns off every rule, hiding problems nobody meant to accept.

SonarQube: S7724

Noncompliant:

```javascript
/* eslint-disable */
f();
```

Compliant:

```javascript
/* eslint-disable no-console */
// eslint-disable-next-line no-alert -- legacy prompt
f(); // see the eslint-disable docs
```

## js/init-to-undefined

**Variables should not be initialized to undefined.** `let x = undefined;` is what `let x;` already does.

SonarQube: S6645

Noncompliant:

```javascript
let current = undefined;
```

Compliant:

```javascript
let current;
const none = undefined;
```

## js/trim-left-right

**trimStart() and trimEnd() should be used instead of trimLeft() and trimRight().** trimLeft() and trimRight() are legacy aliases kept for compatibility.

SonarQube: S7783

Noncompliant:

```javascript
const s = input.trimLeft();
```

Compliant:

```javascript
const s = input.trimStart();
```

## js/jsx-comment-as-text

**Comments in JSX should be inside braces.** In JSX, `// note` between tags is text, not a comment: it's rendered on the page.

SonarQube: S6438

Noncompliant:

```javascript
const v = <div>
  // the user's name
  {name}
</div>;
```

Compliant:

```javascript
const v = <div>
  {/* the user's name */}
  {name}
</div>;
const u = <a href="x">see http://example.com</a>;
```

## js/jsx-missing-key

**Elements rendered from a list should have a key.** React uses `key` to match list items between renders. Without it, items can lose their state or be re-rendered needlessly when the list changes.

SonarQube: S6477

Noncompliant:

```javascript
const v = <ul>{rows.map((r) => <li>{r.name}</li>)}</ul>;
```

Compliant:

```javascript
const v = <ul>{rows.map((r) => <li key={r.id}>{r.name}</li>)}</ul>;
const w = rows.map((r) => { return <Row key={r.id} {...r} />; });
```

## js/jsx-index-key

**Array indexes should not be used as keys.** An index identifies a position, not an item: when the list is reordered, filtered or prepended, React matches the wrong items and their state moves with the position.

SonarQube: S6479

Noncompliant:

```javascript
const v = rows.map((r, i) => <li key={i}>{r.name}</li>);
```

Compliant:

```javascript
const v = rows.map((r, i) => <li key={r.id}>{i + 1}. {r.name}</li>);
```

## js/children-with-inner-html

**children and dangerouslySetInnerHTML should not be used together.** React throws when an element has both children and dangerouslySetInnerHTML.

SonarQube: S6761

Noncompliant:

```javascript
const v = <div dangerouslySetInnerHTML={{ __html: html }}>fallback</div>;
```

Compliant:

```javascript
const v = <div dangerouslySetInnerHTML={{ __html: html }} />;
```

## js/children-prop

**children should be nested, not passed as a prop.** `<Box children={x} />` hides the content in an attribute; nesting it is the idiom readers expect, and both together conflict.

SonarQube: S6748

Noncompliant:

```javascript
const v = <Box children={<Title />} />;
```

Compliant:

```javascript
const v = <Box><Title /></Box>;
```

## js/redundant-fragment

**Fragments around a single element should be removed.** A fragment exists to group several children; around one element it only adds nesting. Fragments with a key are needed and fine.

SonarQube: S6749

Noncompliant:

```javascript
const v = <><Header /></>;
```

Compliant:

```javascript
const v = <><Header /><Body /></>;
const w = <Fragment key={id}><Row /></Fragment>;
const t = <>{label}</>;
```

## js/find-dom-node

**findDOMNode should not be used.** findDOMNode breaks component abstraction, is deprecated, and is removed in React 19. A ref on the element gives the DOM node.

SonarQube: S6788

Noncompliant:

```javascript
const node = ReactDOM.findDOMNode(this);
```

Compliant:

```javascript
const node = this.ref.current;
```

## js/is-mounted

**isMounted should not be used.** `this.isMounted()` is deprecated and hides leaks: cancel subscriptions and requests in componentWillUnmount instead.

SonarQube: S6789

Noncompliant:

```javascript
if (this.isMounted()) { this.setState({ data }); }
```

Compliant:

```javascript
componentWillUnmount() { this.request.abort(); }
```

## js/string-ref

**String refs should not be used.** `ref="name"` is a legacy API, removed in React 19. Use useRef or createRef, or a callback ref.

SonarQube: S6790

Noncompliant:

```javascript
const v = <input ref="name" />;
```

Compliant:

```javascript
const v = <input ref={nameRef} />;
```

## js/legacy-lifecycle

**Legacy React lifecycle methods should not be used.** componentWillMount, componentWillReceiveProps and componentWillUpdate are unsafe with async rendering and removed in React 19.

SonarQube: S6791

Noncompliant:

```javascript
class A extends Component { componentWillMount() { this.load(); } }
```

Compliant:

```javascript
class A extends Component { componentDidMount() { this.load(); } }
```

## js/state-mutation

**this.state should not be changed directly.** Assigning to `this.state` outside the constructor doesn't re-render and is overwritten by the next setState.

SonarQube: S6746

Noncompliant:

```javascript
class A extends Component { onClick() { this.state.count = 1; } }
```

Compliant:

```javascript
class A extends Component { constructor(p) { super(p); this.state = { count: 0 }; } onClick() { this.setState({ count: 1 }); } }
```

## js/set-state-from-state

**setState should use a callback when it reads this.state.** State updates are batched, so `this.state` may already be stale when the update applies. The callback form receives the current state.

SonarQube: S6756

Noncompliant:

```javascript
this.setState({ count: this.state.count + 1 });
```

Compliant:

```javascript
this.setState((prev) => ({ count: prev.count + 1 }));
this.setState({ open: true });
```

## js/state-setter-same-value

**A state setter should not be called with its own state.** `setCount(count)` sets the state to the value it already has: nothing happens. Another value was probably meant.

SonarQube: S6443

Noncompliant:

```javascript
function C() { const [open, setOpen] = useState(false); return <b onClick={() => setOpen(open)} />; }
```

Compliant:

```javascript
function C() { const [open, setOpen] = useState(false); return <b onClick={() => setOpen(!open)} />; }
```

## js/set-state-in-render

**State setters should not be called while rendering.** Calling a useState setter in the component body triggers another render, which calls it again: an infinite loop. Call it from an event handler or an effect.

SonarQube: S6442

Noncompliant:

```javascript
function C({ v }) { const [x, setX] = useState(0); setX(v); return x; }
```

Compliant:

```javascript
function C({ v }) { const [x, setX] = useState(0); useEffect(() => { setX(v); }, [v]); return x; }
```

## js/use-state-naming

**useState results should be destructured as [value, setValue].** The `[thing, setThing]` convention tells readers which setter belongs to which state at a glance.

SonarQube: S6754

Noncompliant:

```javascript
const [count, update] = useState(0);
```

Compliant:

```javascript
const [count, setCount] = useState(0);
const [, setTick] = useState(0);
```

## js/hook-in-condition

**Hooks should not be called conditionally or in loops.** React matches hooks by call order, so a hook inside an if, a loop, a ternary or after `&&` gets another hook's state when the condition changes.

SonarQube: S6440 (related)

Noncompliant:

```javascript
function C({ id }) { if (id) { useEffect(() => load(id)); } }
```

Compliant:

```javascript
function C({ id }) { useEffect(() => { if (id) { load(id); } }); const f = () => (ok ? 1 : 2); }
```

## js/render-without-return

**React render methods should return a value.** A class component's render() that never returns anything renders nothing; usually a `return` was forgotten. Return null to render nothing on purpose.

SonarQube: S6435

Noncompliant:

```javascript
class A extends React.Component { render() { <div />; } }
```

Compliant:

```javascript
class A extends React.Component { render() { return <div />; } }
class Chart { render() { this.draw(); } }
```

## js/react-dom-render-result

**The return value of ReactDOM.render should not be used.** ReactDOM.render's return value is a legacy, unreliable reference. Use a callback ref on the root element instead.

SonarQube: S6750

Noncompliant:

```javascript
const app = ReactDOM.render(<App />, root);
```

Compliant:

```javascript
ReactDOM.render(<App />, root);
```

## js/pure-component-scu

**PureComponent should not define shouldComponentUpdate.** PureComponent exists to implement shouldComponentUpdate with a shallow comparison; defining it again overrides that.

SonarQube: S6763

Noncompliant:

```javascript
class A extends React.PureComponent { shouldComponentUpdate() { return true; } }
```

Compliant:

```javascript
class A extends React.Component { shouldComponentUpdate() { return true; } }
```

## js/img-alt

**Images should have alternative text.** Screen readers read `alt` in place of an image. Without it they read the file name. Use alt="" for decorative images.

SonarQube: S1077

Noncompliant:

```javascript
const v = <img src={logo} />;
```

Compliant:

```javascript
const v = <img src={logo} alt="Company logo" />;
const d = <img src={line} alt="" />;
const p = <img {...props} />;
const i = <input type="text" />;
```

## js/iframe-title

**iframes should have a title.** Screen readers announce an iframe by its title; without one, users can't tell what it contains.

SonarQube: S1090

Noncompliant:

```javascript
const v = <iframe src={url} />;
```

Compliant:

```javascript
const v = <iframe src={url} title="Payment form" />;
```

## js/accesskey

**accessKey should not be used.** Access keys conflict with shortcuts of browsers and assistive technologies, and aren't announced to users.

SonarQube: S6846

Noncompliant:

```javascript
const v = <button accessKey="s">Save</button>;
```

Compliant:

```javascript
const v = <button>Save</button>;
const w = <Shortcut accessKey="s" />;
```

## js/tabindex-positive

**tabIndex should be 0 or -1.** A positive tabIndex moves the element ahead of the page's natural order, which confuses keyboard users.

SonarQube: S6841

Noncompliant:

```javascript
const v = <div tabIndex={2} />;
```

Compliant:

```javascript
const v = <div tabIndex={0} />;
const w = <div tabIndex="-1" />;
```

## js/aria-hidden-focusable

**Focusable elements should not be aria-hidden.** A focusable element hidden from screen readers can still receive focus, leaving users on something that isn't announced.

SonarQube: S6825

Noncompliant:

```javascript
const v = <button aria-hidden="true">x</button>;
```

Compliant:

```javascript
const v = <span aria-hidden="true">x</span>;
const w = <button aria-hidden="true" tabIndex={-1}>x</button>;
```

## js/anchor-as-button

**Links should not be used as buttons.** An `<a>` with an onClick and no real href doesn't behave like a button for keyboard and screen-reader users, and breaks opening in a new tab.

SonarQube: S6844

Noncompliant:

```javascript
const v = <a href="#" onClick={save}>Save</a>;
```

Compliant:

```javascript
const v = <button onClick={save}>Save</button>;
const w = <a href="/docs" onClick={track}>Docs</a>;
```

## js/anchor-content

**Links should have content.** A link with no text and no aria-label is announced as just "link"; users can't tell where it goes.

SonarQube: S6827

Noncompliant:

```javascript
const v = <a href="/home"></a>;
```

Compliant:

```javascript
const v = <a href="/home">Home</a>;
const w = <a href="/home" aria-label="Home" />;
```

## js/heading-content

**Headings should have content.** Screen-reader users navigate by headings; an empty heading is a stop that says nothing.

SonarQube: S6850

Noncompliant:

```javascript
const v = <h2 className="title"></h2>;
```

Compliant:

```javascript
const v = <h2 className="title">{title}</h2>;
```

## js/redundant-alt

**Alternative text should not say it's an image.** Screen readers already announce an image as one; "image of" or "photo" in alt text repeats that.

SonarQube: S6851

Noncompliant:

```javascript
const v = <img src={p} alt="Photo of the team" />;
```

Compliant:

```javascript
const v = <img src={p} alt="The team at the 2026 offsite" />;
const w = <img src={p} alt="Photographer at work" />;
```

## js/mouse-without-keyboard

**Mouse events should have keyboard equivalents.** onMouseOver and onMouseOut only fire for mouse users; keyboard users need the same behavior on onFocus and onBlur.

SonarQube: S1082

Noncompliant:

```javascript
const v = <div onMouseOver={show}>i</div>;
```

Compliant:

```javascript
const v = <div onMouseOver={show} onFocus={show}>i</div>;
const w = <Tip onMouseOver={show} />;
```

## js/test-empty-title

**Tests and suites should have a title.** An empty title makes failures impossible to locate in the report.

SonarQube: S8781

Noncompliant:

```javascript
it('', () => { expect(sum(1, 2)).toBe(3); });
```

Compliant:

```javascript
it('adds two numbers', () => { expect(sum(1, 2)).toBe(3); });
```

## js/async-describe

**Suite callbacks should be synchronous.** describe() collects tests synchronously and doesn't wait for a returned promise: tests registered after an await are lost.

SonarQube: S8785

Noncompliant:

```javascript
describe('db', async () => { const db = await open(); it('reads', () => {}); });
```

Compliant:

```javascript
describe('db', () => { let db; beforeAll(async () => { db = await open(); }); it('reads', async () => { await db.read(); }); });
```

## js/assertion-same-argument

**Assertions should not compare a value with itself.** `expect(x).toBe(x)` always passes; one side was meant to be the expected value.

SonarQube: S5863

Noncompliant:

```javascript
expect(total).toBe(total);
assert.equal(a.length, a.length);
```

Compliant:

```javascript
expect(total).toBe(10);
assert.equal(a.length, b.length);
```

## js/assertion-constant

**Assertions should not compare constants.** `expect(true).toBe(true)` or `assert(true)` can't fail, so they test nothing.

SonarQube: S5914

Noncompliant:

```javascript
expect(true).toBe(true);
```

Compliant:

```javascript
expect(isReady()).toBe(true);
assert.fail('unreachable');
```

## js/assertion-order

**Assertions should put the actual value first.** expect(actual).toBe(expected) and assert.equal(actual, expected): swapping them gives failure messages that say the opposite of what happened.

SonarQube: S3415

Noncompliant:

```javascript
expect(3).toBe(sum(1, 2));
assert.equal(3, sum(1, 2));
```

Compliant:

```javascript
expect(sum(1, 2)).toBe(3);
assert.equal(sum(1, 2), 3);
```

## js/assertion-specific

**The most specific assertion should be used.** `expect(a.length).toBe(3)` reports "expected 3, got 2"; `toHaveLength(3)` also shows the array. Likewise `expect(a === b)` and `assert(a === b)` lose the values being compared.

SonarQube: S5906

Noncompliant:

```javascript
expect(items.length).toBe(3);
assert(a === b);
```

Compliant:

```javascript
expect(items).toHaveLength(3);
assert.strictEqual(a, b);
```

## js/incomplete-assertion

**Assertions should be complete.** `expect(x);` or `expect(x).not;` asserts nothing: the matcher call is missing, so the test can't fail.

SonarQube: S2970

Noncompliant:

```javascript
expect(result).not;
```

Compliant:

```javascript
expect(result).not.toBeNull();
expect(flag).to.be.true;
```

## js/unspecified-throw

**Tests should check which error is thrown.** `toThrow()` with no argument passes for any error, including a TypeError from a typo in the test itself.

SonarQube: S5958

Noncompliant:

```javascript
expect(() => parse('')).toThrow();
```

Compliant:

```javascript
expect(() => parse('')).toThrow(SyntaxError);
assert.throws(() => parse(''), /empty/);
```

## js/async-assertion-not-awaited

**Assertions on promises should be awaited.** `expect(p).resolves...` returns a promise. If it isn't awaited or returned, the test ends before it settles and passes regardless.

SonarQube: S8780

Noncompliant:

```javascript
it('loads', () => { expect(load()).resolves.toBe(1); });
```

Compliant:

```javascript
it('loads', async () => { await expect(load()).resolves.toBe(1); });
it('fails', () => expect(load()).rejects.toThrow(E));
```

## js/skipped-test-without-reason

**Skipped tests should say why.** A skipped test with no explanation tends to stay skipped forever; a comment saying why (and when to re-enable it) keeps it honest.

SonarQube: S1607

Noncompliant:

```javascript
it.skip('syncs', () => {});
```

Compliant:

```javascript
// Flaky on CI until the mock server is fixed (#123).
it.skip('syncs', () => {});
```

## js/duplicate-test-title

**Test titles should be unique within a suite.** Two tests with the same title in one suite can't be told apart in reports, and one is often a copy that was never renamed.

SonarQube: S8754

Noncompliant:

```javascript
describe('sum', () => {
  it('adds', () => {});
  it('adds', () => {});
});
```

Compliant:

```javascript
describe('sum', () => {
  it('adds', () => {});
  it('adds negatives', () => {});
});
describe('other', () => { it('adds', () => {}); });
```

## js/hook-after-test

**Setup and teardown hooks should come before the tests.** beforeEach and friends apply to every test in the suite regardless of position; placed after tests, they read as if they only applied to what follows.

SonarQube: S8782

Noncompliant:

```javascript
describe('db', () => {
  it('reads', () => {});
  beforeEach(() => reset());
});
```

Compliant:

```javascript
describe('db', () => {
  beforeEach(() => reset());
  it('reads', () => {});
});
```

## js/fixed-wait

**Tests should not use fixed waits or debug pauses.** `cy.wait(1000)` or `page.waitForTimeout(500)` makes tests slow and flaky; `cy.pause()` and `page.pause()` stop a CI run.

SonarQube: S2925

Noncompliant:

```javascript
cy.wait(2000);
```

Compliant:

```javascript
cy.wait('@save');
cy.get('[data-test=done]').should('be.visible');
```

## js/float-equality

**Floating-point numbers should not be tested for equality.** Decimal fractions like 0.1 aren't exact in binary, so `0.1 + 0.2 === 0.3` is false. Compare within a tolerance instead.

SonarQube: S1244

Noncompliant:

```javascript
if (total === 0.3) { done(); }
```

Compliant:

```javascript
if (Math.abs(total - 0.3) < 1e-9) { done(); }
if (n === 3) { done(); }
const half = 0.5;
```

## js/new-result-dropped

**Objects should not be created only to be dropped.** `new Thing();` on its own line throws the object away. If the constructor is called for its side effects, a function would say so; otherwise the result was meant to be stored. Errors are the error-not-thrown rule's case, and `new URL(s)` inside a try block (to validate) is fine.

SonarQube: S1848

Noncompliant:

```javascript
new Logger(config);
```

Compliant:

```javascript
const logger = new Logger(config);
new Error('x');
```

## js/single-iteration-loop

**Loops should not always exit on their first iteration.** A loop whose body always ends in return, throw or break runs at most once: either the exit is misplaced or it should be an if.

SonarQube: S1751

Noncompliant:

```javascript
for (const user of users) { notify(user); return; }
```

Compliant:

```javascript
for (const user of users) { if (user.admin) { return user; } }
while (q.length) { const x = q.pop(); if (!x) { continue; } return x; }
```

## js/unreachable-code

**Code after return, throw, break or continue should be removed.** Statements after an unconditional jump never run. Function declarations are hoisted and fine.

SonarQube: S1763 (related)

Noncompliant:

```javascript
function f() { return 1; cleanup(); }
```

Compliant:

```javascript
function f() { cleanup(); return 1; function helper() {} }
```

## js/useless-expression

**Statements should have an effect.** `a === b;` or `x;` computes a value and drops it: usually an assignment, call or return was meant.

SonarQube: S905

Noncompliant:

```javascript
function reset(state) { state.count === 0; }
```

Compliant:

```javascript
function reset(state) { state.count = 0; }
ready && start();
void run();
```

## js/super-call

**super() should be called in derived constructors, and only there.** A derived class's constructor must call super() before using `this`; a class that extends nothing can't call it at all.

SonarQube: S3854 (related)

Noncompliant:

```javascript
class Admin extends User { constructor(name) { this.role = 'admin'; } }
```

Compliant:

```javascript
class Admin extends User { constructor(name) { super(name); this.role = 'admin'; } }
class Box { constructor() { this.items = []; } }
```

## js/overwritten-element

**Elements should not be overwritten right after being set.** `a[k] = x; a[k] = y;` or two `map.set(k, ...)` in a row discard the first value: one key is probably wrong.

SonarQube: S4143

Noncompliant:

```javascript
names[0] = 'a';
names[0] = 'b';
```

Compliant:

```javascript
names[0] = 'a';
names[1] = 'b';
count[k] = 1;
count[k] = count[k] + 1;
```

## js/constant-comparison

**Comparisons should not have a constant result.** `x === {}` compares with a brand-new object, so it's always false, and `'a' ?? b` never uses `b`.

SonarQube: S6638 (related)

Noncompliant:

```javascript
if (options === {}) { useDefaults(); }
```

Compliant:

```javascript
if (Object.keys(options).length === 0) { useDefaults(); }
const n = value ?? 0;
```

## js/accessor-recursion

**Getters and setters should not access their own property.** `get name() { return this.name; }` calls itself until the stack overflows; the value belongs in another field, like `this._name`.

SonarQube: S7725

Noncompliant:

```javascript
class U { get name() { return this.name; } }
```

Compliant:

```javascript
class U { get name() { return this._name; } set name(v) { this._name = v; } }
```

## js/get-with-body

**GET and HEAD requests should not have a body.** Browsers reject a body on GET and HEAD (fetch throws), and servers may ignore it.

SonarQube: S7733

Noncompliant:

```javascript
fetch(url, { method: 'GET', body: JSON.stringify(q) });
```

Compliant:

```javascript
fetch(url, { method: 'POST', body: JSON.stringify(q) });
```

## js/then-property

**Objects should not have a then property.** An object with a `then` method is treated as a promise: `await obj` or returning it from an async function calls it.

SonarQube: S7739

Noncompliant:

```javascript
const step = { then: () => next() };
```

Compliant:

```javascript
const step = { andThen: () => next() };
p.then(f);
```

## js/infinite-loop

**Loops should be able to end.** `while (true)` or `for (;;)` without a break, return or throw never ends. Loops that await or yield are left alone: they wait between iterations on purpose.

SonarQube: S2189 (related)

Noncompliant:

```javascript
while (true) { tick(); }
```

Compliant:

```javascript
while (true) { if (tick()) { break; } }
while (true) { await next(); }
for (;;) { for (;;) { break; } return; }
```

## js/unsafe-optional-chaining

**Optional chaining should not be used where undefined throws.** `(a?.b).c`, `(a?.f)()`, `[...a?.list]` and `for (x of a?.list)` throw a TypeError when the chain short-circuits, which defeats the `?.`.

SonarQube: S6523

Noncompliant:

```javascript
const n = (user?.address).street;
```

Compliant:

```javascript
const n = user?.address?.street;
const all = [...(user?.tags ?? [])];
```

## js/weak-tls

**Weak TLS versions should not be allowed.** SSL 3, TLS 1.0 and TLS 1.1 have known attacks. Require TLS 1.2 or later.

SonarQube: S4423

Standards: [CWE-295](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/295.html), [CWE-326](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/326.html), [CWE-327](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/327.html), OWASP A04:2025 Cryptographic Failures, OWASP A07:2025 Authentication Failures, OWASP A02:2021 Cryptographic Failures, OWASP A07:2021 Identification and Authentication Failures

Noncompliant:

```javascript
tls.connect({ host, minVersion: 'TLSv1' });
```

Compliant:

```javascript
tls.connect({ host, minVersion: 'TLSv1.2' });
```

## js/weak-key

**Cryptographic keys should be long enough.** RSA and DSA keys shorter than 2048 bits, and elliptic curves under 224 bits, can be broken.

SonarQube: S4426

Standards: [CWE-326](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/326.html), OWASP A04:2025 Cryptographic Failures, OWASP A02:2021 Cryptographic Failures

Noncompliant:

```javascript
crypto.generateKeyPairSync('rsa', { modulusLength: 1024 });
```

Compliant:

```javascript
crypto.generateKeyPairSync('rsa', { modulusLength: 4096 });
crypto.generateKeyPairSync('ec', { namedCurve: 'P-256' });
```

## js/tls-hostname-unverified

**TLS server hostnames should be verified.** A checkServerIdentity that accepts everything lets any certificate for any host through, so anyone on the path can impersonate the server.

SonarQube: S5527

Standards: [CWE-297](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/297.html), OWASP A07:2025 Authentication Failures, OWASP A02:2021 Cryptographic Failures, OWASP A05:2021 Security Misconfiguration, OWASP A07:2021 Identification and Authentication Failures

Noncompliant:

```javascript
https.request({ host, checkServerIdentity: () => {} });
```

Compliant:

```javascript
https.request({ host, checkServerIdentity: (h, cert) => pinned(cert) ? undefined : new Error('pin') });
```

## js/weak-cipher

**Weak ciphers should not be used.** DES, 3DES, RC2, RC4 and Blowfish have small keys or blocks and practical attacks. AES is the standard choice.

SonarQube: S5547

Standards: [CWE-326](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/326.html), [CWE-327](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/327.html), OWASP A04:2025 Cryptographic Failures, OWASP A02:2021 Cryptographic Failures

Noncompliant:

```javascript
crypto.createCipheriv('des-ede3-cbc', key, iv);
```

Compliant:

```javascript
crypto.createCipheriv('aes-256-gcm', key, iv);
```

## js/insecure-cipher-mode

**Encryption should use a secure mode and padding.** ECB leaks patterns in the data, and CBC (and RSA's PKCS#1 v1.5 padding) allows padding-oracle attacks. Use GCM, or OAEP for RSA.

SonarQube: S5542

Standards: [CWE-327](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/327.html), [CWE-780](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/780.html), OWASP A04:2025 Cryptographic Failures, OWASP A02:2021 Cryptographic Failures

Noncompliant:

```javascript
crypto.createCipheriv('aes-128-ecb', key, null);
```

Compliant:

```javascript
crypto.createCipheriv('aes-128-gcm', key, iv);
```

## js/jwt-none

**JWTs should be signed and verified with a real algorithm.** The 'none' algorithm means unsigned tokens: anyone can forge one, and a verifier that accepts it trusts them.

SonarQube: S5659

Standards: [CWE-347](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/347.html), OWASP A04:2025 Cryptographic Failures, OWASP A02:2021 Cryptographic Failures

Noncompliant:

```javascript
jwt.verify(token, key, { algorithms: ['RS256', 'none'] });
```

Compliant:

```javascript
jwt.verify(token, key, { algorithms: ['RS256'] });
```

## js/world-permissions

**Files should not be made writable by everyone.** A mode that lets "others" write (like 0o777 or 0o666) lets every user on the machine change the file. Read and execute for others (0o755) are left alone: executables and public files need them.

SonarQube: S2612 (related)

Standards: [CWE-266](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/266.html), [CWE-732](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/732.html), OWASP A01:2025 Broken Access Control, OWASP A06:2025 Insecure Design, OWASP A01:2021 Broken Access Control, OWASP A04:2021 Insecure Design

Noncompliant:

```javascript
fs.chmodSync(file, 0o777);
```

Compliant:

```javascript
fs.chmodSync(file, 0o750);
fs.chmodSync(bin, 0o755);
```

## js/public-tmp

**Files should not be created in shared temporary directories by name.** Paths like /tmp/app.log can be guessed: another user can create or link the file first. Use fs.mkdtemp() under os.tmpdir().

SonarQube: S5443

Standards: [CWE-377](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/377.html), [CWE-379](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/379.html), OWASP A01:2025 Broken Access Control, OWASP A01:2021 Broken Access Control

Noncompliant:

```javascript
fs.writeFileSync('/tmp/report.csv', data);
```

Compliant:

```javascript
const dir = fs.mkdtempSync(path.join(os.tmpdir(), 'report-'));
```

## js/window-open-opener

**Windows opened by window.open should not get access to the opener.** Without 'noopener', the opened page can navigate this one through window.opener (reverse tabnabbing).

SonarQube: S5148

Standards: [CWE-1022](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1022.html), OWASP A06:2025 Insecure Design, OWASP A05:2021 Security Misconfiguration

Noncompliant:

```javascript
window.open(link, '_blank');
```

Compliant:

```javascript
window.open(link, '_blank', 'noopener,noreferrer');
```

## js/jsx-target-blank

**Links opening a new tab should not give it access to this page.** `target="_blank"` to another site without `rel="noopener"` lets that page navigate this one through window.opener in older browsers.

SonarQube: S5148

Standards: [CWE-1022](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1022.html), OWASP A06:2025 Insecure Design, OWASP A05:2021 Security Misconfiguration

Noncompliant:

```javascript
const v = <a href="https://example.com" target="_blank">Docs</a>;
```

Compliant:

```javascript
const v = <a href="https://example.com" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Docs</a>;
const w = <a href="/help" target="_blank">Help</a>;
```

## js/command-path

**OS commands should be run by absolute path.** A bare command name is looked up in PATH, which whoever controls the environment can point at another program.

SonarQube: S4036

Standards: [CWE-426](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/426.html), [CWE-427](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/427.html), OWASP A08:2025 Software or Data Integrity Failures, OWASP A08:2021 Software and Data Integrity Failures

Noncompliant:

```javascript
child_process.execSync('git status');
```

Compliant:

```javascript
child_process.execFileSync('/usr/bin/git', ['status']);
const m = re.exec(line);
```

## js/hardcoded-password

**Passwords should not be hard-coded.** A password in the source is visible to everyone with access to the repository, and stays in its history. Read it from the environment or a secret store.

SonarQube: S2068, S6437 (related)

Standards: [CWE-259](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/259.html), [CWE-798](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/798.html), OWASP A07:2025 Authentication Failures, OWASP A07:2021 Identification and Authentication Failures

Noncompliant:

```javascript
const db = connect({ user: 'app', password: 'hunter2' });
```

Compliant:

```javascript
const db = connect({ user: 'app', password: process.env.DB_PASSWORD });
const labels = { password: 'Your password' };
const field = { password: '' };
```

## js/hardcoded-secret

**Secrets should not be hard-coded.** Tokens and API keys in the source leak with every copy of the repository. Only random-looking values are flagged: at least 16 characters, mixing letters and digits.

SonarQube: S6418

Standards: [CWE-798](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/798.html), OWASP A07:2025 Authentication Failures, OWASP A07:2021 Identification and Authentication Failures

Noncompliant:

```javascript
const apiKey = 'x9Fq2LmZ7vR4tK8wB1nC6pD3';
```

Compliant:

```javascript
const apiKey = process.env.API_KEY;
const tokenHeader = 'Authorization';
```

## js/xxe

**XML parsers should not expand external entities.** With `noent: true`, libxmljs resolves external entities, so a crafted document can read local files or reach internal hosts (XXE).

SonarQube: S2755

Standards: [CWE-611](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/611.html), [CWE-827](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/827.html), OWASP A02:2025 Security Misconfiguration, OWASP A05:2021 Security Misconfiguration

Noncompliant:

```javascript
libxmljs.parseXml(xml, { noent: true });
```

Compliant:

```javascript
libxmljs.parseXml(xml, { noblanks: true });
```

## js/cookie-not-secure

**Cookies should have the secure flag.** Without `secure`, the cookie is also sent over plain HTTP, where anyone on the network can read it.

SonarQube: S2092

Standards: [CWE-311](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/311.html), [CWE-315](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/315.html), [CWE-614](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/614.html), OWASP A02:2025 Security Misconfiguration, OWASP A06:2025 Insecure Design, OWASP A04:2021 Insecure Design, OWASP A05:2021 Security Misconfiguration

Noncompliant:

```javascript
res.cookie('sid', id, { secure: false });
```

Compliant:

```javascript
res.cookie('sid', id, { secure: true, httpOnly: true });
nodemailer.createTransport({ host, secure: false });
```

## js/cookie-not-httponly

**Cookies should have the HttpOnly flag.** Without `httpOnly`, scripts on the page can read the cookie, so any XSS can steal the session.

SonarQube: S3330

Standards: [CWE-1004](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1004.html), OWASP A02:2025 Security Misconfiguration, OWASP A05:2021 Security Misconfiguration

Noncompliant:

```javascript
app.use(session({ secret, cookie: { httpOnly: false } }));
```

Compliant:

```javascript
app.use(session({ secret, cookie: { httpOnly: true, secure: true } }));
```

## js/cors-any-origin

**CORS should not allow every origin.** `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` (also cors() with no options) lets any website read this server's responses from a visitor's browser.

SonarQube: S5122

Standards: [CWE-346](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/346.html), [CWE-942](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/942.html), OWASP A02:2025 Security Misconfiguration, OWASP A07:2025 Authentication Failures, OWASP A05:2021 Security Misconfiguration, OWASP A07:2021 Identification and Authentication Failures

Noncompliant:

```javascript
res.setHeader('Access-Control-Allow-Origin', '*');
```

Compliant:

```javascript
res.setHeader('Access-Control-Allow-Origin', 'https://app.example.com');
app.use(cors({ origin: ['https://app.example.com'] }));
```

## js/csp-disabled

**The Content-Security-Policy should not be disabled.** helmet's CSP limits where scripts and other content can load from; turning it off removes a key defense against XSS.

SonarQube: S5728

Standards: [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html), OWASP A05:2025 Injection, OWASP A05:2021 Security Misconfiguration

Noncompliant:

```javascript
app.use(helmet({ contentSecurityPolicy: false }));
```

Compliant:

```javascript
app.use(helmet({ contentSecurityPolicy: { directives: { defaultSrc: ["'self'"] } } }));
```

## js/nosniff-disabled

**MIME type sniffing should stay disabled.** Without `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, browsers may run an upload or response as a script despite its declared type.

SonarQube: S5734

Standards: [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html), [CWE-436](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/436.html), OWASP A05:2025 Injection, OWASP A06:2025 Insecure Design, OWASP A05:2021 Security Misconfiguration

Noncompliant:

```javascript
app.use(helmet({ noSniff: false }));
```

Compliant:

```javascript
app.use(helmet());
```

## js/unsafe-referrer-policy

**The Referrer-Policy should not leak full URLs.** 'unsafe-url' and 'no-referrer-when-downgrade' send the full URL, including paths and query strings, to other sites.

SonarQube: S5736

Standards: [CWE-200](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/200.html), OWASP A01:2025 Broken Access Control, OWASP A01:2021 Broken Access Control

Noncompliant:

```javascript
app.use(helmet.referrerPolicy({ policy: 'unsafe-url' }));
```

Compliant:

```javascript
app.use(helmet.referrerPolicy({ policy: 'no-referrer' }));
```

## js/hsts-disabled

**HTTP Strict-Transport-Security should not be disabled.** HSTS tells browsers to use HTTPS only; without it, a first plain-HTTP request can be intercepted and downgraded.

SonarQube: S5739

Standards: [CWE-319](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/319.html), [CWE-693](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/693.html), OWASP A04:2025 Cryptographic Failures, OWASP A06:2025 Insecure Design, OWASP A05:2021 Security Misconfiguration

Noncompliant:

```javascript
app.use(helmet({ hsts: false }));
```

Compliant:

```javascript
app.use(helmet({ hsts: { maxAge: 31536000 } }));
```

## ts/no-explicit-any

**The 'any' type should not be used.** `any` disables type checking; prefer `unknown` or a precise type.

SonarQube: S4204

Noncompliant:

```typescript
let x: any = 1;
```

Compliant:

```typescript
let x: unknown = 1;
```

## js/angular-sanitizer-bypass

**Angular's built-in sanitization should not be bypassed.** bypassSecurityTrust* tells Angular to render a value without sanitizing it, so any attacker-controlled text in it runs as script.

SonarQube: S6268

Standards: [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html), OWASP A05:2025 Injection, OWASP A03:2021 Injection

Noncompliant:

```typescript
const html = this.sanitizer.bypassSecurityTrustHtml(userInput);
```

Compliant:

```typescript
const html = this.sanitizer.sanitize(SecurityContext.HTML, userInput);
```

## js/mocha-timeout-disabled

**Mocha timeouts should not be disabled silently.** this.timeout(0) removes the time limit, so a hung test blocks the whole run. Use a large, explicit value so the intent is visible.

SonarQube: S6080

Noncompliant:

```javascript
it('syncs', function () { this.timeout(0); });
```

Compliant:

```javascript
it('syncs', function () { this.timeout(60000); });
```

## js/error-handler-middleware

**Debugging middleware should not run in production.** The errorhandler package sends stack traces and internals to the client, which helps attackers map the application.

SonarQube: S4507

Standards: [CWE-215](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/215.html), [CWE-489](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/489.html), OWASP A02:2025 Security Misconfiguration, OWASP A10:2025 Mishandling of Exceptional Conditions, OWASP A05:2021 Security Misconfiguration

Noncompliant:

```javascript
app.use(errorhandler());
```

Compliant:

```javascript
if (process.env.NODE_ENV === 'development') { app.use(errorhandler()); }
```

## js/csrf-methods-ignored

**CSRF protection should cover state-changing methods.** Listing POST, PUT, PATCH or DELETE in csurf's ignoreMethods turns off CSRF protection for exactly the requests it exists to protect.

SonarQube: S4502 (related)

Standards: [CWE-352](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/352.html), OWASP A01:2025 Broken Access Control, OWASP A01:2021 Broken Access Control

Noncompliant:

```javascript
app.use(csurf({ ignoreMethods: ['GET', 'POST'] }));
```

Compliant:

```javascript
app.use(csurf({ ignoreMethods: ['GET', 'HEAD', 'OPTIONS'] }));
```

## js/upload-without-limit

**File uploads should have a size limit.** Without a limit, one request can fill the disk or memory of the server.

SonarQube: S2598 (related)

Standards: [CWE-400](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/400.html), [CWE-434](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/434.html), OWASP A06:2025 Insecure Design, OWASP A04:2021 Insecure Design

Noncompliant:

```javascript
const upload = multer({ dest: 'uploads/' });
```

Compliant:

```javascript
const upload = multer({ dest: 'uploads/', limits: { fileSize: 1000000 } });
```

## js/template-built-dynamically

**Templates should not be built from strings at runtime.** Concatenating or interpolating data into the template source lets an attacker inject template code, which usually means code execution.

SonarQube: S7790 (related)

Standards: [CWE-20](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/20.html), OWASP A05:2025 Injection, OWASP A03:2021 Injection

Noncompliant:

```javascript
const html = ejs.render('<h1>' + title + '</h1>', data);
```

Compliant:

```javascript
const html = ejs.render('<h1><%= title %></h1>', { title });
```

## js/express-version-disclosure

**Express should not announce its version.** The default X-Powered-By header tells attackers which framework is running, so they can look for known flaws in it.

SonarQube: S5689 (related)

Standards: [CWE-200](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/200.html), OWASP A01:2025 Broken Access Control, OWASP A05:2021 Security Misconfiguration

Noncompliant:

```javascript
const app = express();
```

Compliant:

```javascript
const app = express();
app.disable('x-powered-by');
```

## js/cleartext-url

**Clear-text protocols should not be used.** http, ftp and telnet send data, including credentials, unencrypted. Loopback and private-network addresses, test domains and XML namespaces are not reported.

SonarQube: S5332 (related)

Standards: [CWE-200](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/200.html), [CWE-319](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/319.html), OWASP A01:2025 Broken Access Control, OWASP A04:2025 Cryptographic Failures, OWASP A02:2021 Cryptographic Failures

Noncompliant:

```javascript
const api = 'http://api.example-shop.io/v1';
```

Compliant:

```javascript
const api = 'https://api.example-shop.io/v1';
const dev = 'http://localhost:3000';
```

## js/math-random-secret

**Math.random() should not generate secrets.** Math.random() is predictable, so tokens, passwords and nonces built from it can be guessed. Use crypto.randomBytes or crypto.randomUUID.

SonarQube: S2245 (related)

Standards: [CWE-326](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/326.html), [CWE-330](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/330.html), [CWE-338](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/338.html), [CWE-1241](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1241.html), OWASP A04:2025 Cryptographic Failures, OWASP A02:2021 Cryptographic Failures

Noncompliant:

```javascript
const sessionToken = Math.random().toString(36);
```

Compliant:

```javascript
const sessionToken = crypto.randomBytes(16).toString('hex');
const jitter = Math.random() * 100;
```

## js/this-in-function-component

**"this" should not be used in function components.** A function component has no instance, so `this` is undefined there; read props from the parameter and state from hooks.

SonarQube: S6757

Noncompliant:

```javascript
function Greeting(props) { return <h1>{this.props.name}</h1>; }
```

Compliant:

```javascript
function Greeting(props) { return <h1>{props.name}</h1>; }
```

## js/table-without-headers

**Tables should have headers.** Screen readers announce a cell by its column and row headers; a table without any <th> leaves them guessing.

SonarQube: S5256 (related)

Noncompliant:

```javascript
const t = <table><tbody><tr><td>1</td></tr></tbody></table>;
```

Compliant:

```javascript
const t = <table><thead><tr><th>Id</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>1</td></tr></tbody></table>;
```

## js/upload-size-too-large

**Uploads should be limited to a reasonable size.** A limit above 8 MB lets a few requests exhaust memory or disk. Most uploads (images, documents) fit well below it.

SonarQube: S5693 (related)

Standards: [CWE-400](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/400.html), [CWE-770](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/770.html)

Noncompliant:

```javascript
const upload = multer({ limits: { fileSize: 50000000 } });
```

Compliant:

```javascript
const upload = multer({ limits: { fileSize: 2000000 } });
```

## cdk/bucket-allows-plain-http

**S3 buckets should enforce HTTPS-only access.** Without enforceSSL, the bucket accepts requests over plain HTTP, where data and credentials can be read in transit.

SonarQube: S6249

Standards: [CWE-319](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/319.html), OWASP A04:2025 Cryptographic Failures

Noncompliant:

```typescript
import 'aws-cdk-lib';
const b = new s3.Bucket(this, 'Data', { versioned: true });
```

Compliant:

```typescript
import 'aws-cdk-lib';
const b = new s3.Bucket(this, 'Data', { enforceSSL: true });
```

## cdk/bucket-without-versioning

**S3 buckets should have versioning enabled.** Without versioning, an overwritten or deleted object cannot be recovered.

SonarQube: S6252

Standards: [CWE-16](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/16.html), OWASP A02:2025 Security Misconfiguration

Noncompliant:

```typescript
import 'aws-cdk-lib';
const b = new s3.Bucket(this, 'Data', { enforceSSL: true });
```

Compliant:

```typescript
import 'aws-cdk-lib';
const b = new s3.Bucket(this, 'Data', { versioned: true });
```

## cdk/bucket-public-access

**S3 buckets should not grant access to everyone.** publicReadAccess and the PUBLIC_READ, PUBLIC_READ_WRITE and AUTHENTICATED_READ ACLs let anyone, or any AWS account, read the bucket.

SonarQube: S6265

Standards: [CWE-284](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/284.html), [CWE-732](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/732.html), OWASP A01:2025 Broken Access Control

Noncompliant:

```typescript
import 'aws-cdk-lib';
const b = new s3.Bucket(this, 'Site', { publicReadAccess: true });
```

Compliant:

```typescript
import 'aws-cdk-lib';
const b = new s3.Bucket(this, 'Site', { publicReadAccess: false });
```

## cdk/bucket-public-access-not-blocked

**S3 bucket public access should be fully blocked.** Without blockPublicAccess, a later ACL or policy mistake can make the bucket public.

SonarQube: S6281

Standards: [CWE-284](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/284.html), [CWE-732](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/732.html), OWASP A01:2025 Broken Access Control

Noncompliant:

```typescript
import 'aws-cdk-lib';
const b = new s3.Bucket(this, 'Data', { versioned: true });
```

Compliant:

```typescript
import 'aws-cdk-lib';
const b = new s3.Bucket(this, 'Data', { blockPublicAccess: s3.BlockPublicAccess.BLOCK_ALL });
```

## cdk/volume-not-encrypted

**EBS volumes should be encrypted.** An unencrypted volume exposes its data if a snapshot or the disk is accessed outside the instance.

SonarQube: S6275

Standards: [CWE-311](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/311.html), OWASP A06:2025 Insecure Design

Noncompliant:

```typescript
import 'aws-cdk-lib';
const v = new ec2.Volume(this, 'Disk', { size: Size.gibibytes(10) });
```

Compliant:

```typescript
import 'aws-cdk-lib';
const v = new ec2.Volume(this, 'Disk', { encrypted: true });
```

## cdk/database-not-encrypted

**RDS databases should be encrypted at rest.** Without storage encryption, the database files, backups and snapshots are readable by anyone who gets at the storage.

SonarQube: S6303

Standards: [CWE-311](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/311.html), OWASP A06:2025 Insecure Design

Noncompliant:

```typescript
import 'aws-cdk-lib';
const d = new rds.DatabaseInstance(this, 'Db', { engine });
```

Compliant:

```typescript
import 'aws-cdk-lib';
const d = new rds.DatabaseInstance(this, 'Db', { engine, storageEncrypted: true });
```

## cdk/search-domain-not-encrypted

**OpenSearch domains should be encrypted at rest.** Without encryption at rest, indexed documents are stored in clear text.

SonarQube: S6308

Standards: [CWE-311](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/311.html), OWASP A06:2025 Insecure Design

Noncompliant:

```typescript
import 'aws-cdk-lib';
const d = new opensearch.Domain(this, 'Search', { version });
```

Compliant:

```typescript
import 'aws-cdk-lib';
const d = new opensearch.Domain(this, 'Search', { version, encryptionAtRest: { enabled: true } });
```

## cdk/notebook-not-encrypted

**SageMaker notebook instances should be encrypted at rest.** Without a KMS key, the notebook's storage volume is not encrypted with a key you control.

SonarQube: S6319
